# Northeast Corridor - Wikipedia

[Jump to content](#bodyContent)

From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

Railroad corridor in the Northeastern United States

This article is about the Amtrak main line. For the New Jersey Transit service, see [Northeast Corridor Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Corridor_Line "Northeast Corridor Line"). For the agglomeration of metropolitan areas, see [Northeast megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_megalopolis "Northeast megalopolis"). For the MRT line of Singapore, see [North East Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_East_Line "North East Line").

**Northeast Corridor**

The **Northeast Corridor** ( **NEC**) is an electrified railroad line in the [Northeast megalopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_megalopolis "Northeast megalopolis") of the United States. Owned primarily by [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak "Amtrak"), it runs from [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston "Boston") in the north to [Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D.C. "Washington, D.C."), in the south, with major stops in [Providence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence,_Rhode_Island "Providence, Rhode Island"), [New Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut "New Haven, Connecticut"), [Stamford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamford,_Connecticut "Stamford, Connecticut"), [New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City "New York City"), [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey "Newark, New Jersey"), [Trenton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey"), [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia "Philadelphia"), [Wilmington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmington,_Delaware "Wilmington, Delaware"), and [Baltimore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore "Baltimore"). The NEC is roughly paralleled by [Interstate 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_95 "Interstate 95") for most of its length. The NEC is the busiest passenger rail line in the United States by both ridership and frequency, with more than 2,200 trains a day running over at least some portion of the line.[&#8202;](#ref-back-3-0)[\[2\]](#refnote-3)[&#8202;](#ref-back-4-0)[\[3\]](#refnote-4)

The corridor is used by many Amtrak trains, including the high-speed _[Acela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela "Acela")_ (formerly _Acela Express_), intercity trains, and several [long-distance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long-distance_Amtrak_routes "Long-distance Amtrak routes") trains. Most of the corridor also has frequent [commuter rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commuter_rail_in_North_America "Commuter rail in North America") service, provided by the [MBTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBTA_Commuter_Rail "MBTA Commuter Rail"), [CT Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CT_Rail "CT Rail"), [Metro-North Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro-North_Railroad "Metro-North Railroad"), [Long Island Rail Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island_Rail_Road "Long Island Rail Road"), [New Jersey Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Transit_Rail_Operations "New Jersey Transit Rail Operations"), [SEPTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SEPTA_Regional_Rail "SEPTA Regional Rail"), and [MARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MARC_Train "MARC Train"). While large through freights have not run on the NEC since the early 1980s, some sections still carry smaller local freights operated by [CSX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSX "CSX"), [Norfolk Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern "Norfolk Southern"), [CSAO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail_Shared_Assets_Operations "Conrail Shared Assets Operations"), [Providence and Worcester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence_and_Worcester "Providence and Worcester"), [New York and Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_and_Atlantic "New York and Atlantic"), and [Canadian Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Pacific "Canadian Pacific"). CSX and NS partly own their routes.

[Long-distance Amtrak services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long-distance_Amtrak_routes "Long-distance Amtrak routes") that use the Northeast Corridor include the _[Cardinal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_(Amtrak) "Cardinal (Amtrak)")_, _[Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent_(Amtrak) "Crescent (Amtrak)")_, and _[Silver Meteor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Meteor_(Amtrak) "Silver Meteor (Amtrak)")_ trains, which reach 125mph (201km/h), as well as its _Acela_ trains, which reach 150–160mph (240–260km/h) in parts of [Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts "Massachusetts"), [Rhode Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhode_Island "Rhode Island"), and [New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey "New Jersey"). Some express trains operated by MARC that reach 125mph (201km/h) also operate on the Northeast Corridor. _Acela_ can travel the 225mi (362km) between New York City and Washington, D.C., in under three hours, and the 229mi (369km) between New York and Boston in under 3.5 hours.[&#8202;](#ref-back-5-0)[\[4\]](#refnote-5)[&#8202;](#ref-back-6-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-6)

In 2012, Amtrak proposed improvements to enable "true" high-speed rail on the corridor, which would have roughly halved travel times at an estimated cost of $151 billion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-vision2012-0)[\[6\]](#refnote-vision2012)[&#8202;](#ref-back-8-0)[\[7\]](#refnote-8)

## History

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: History")\]

### Origins

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Origins")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/NEC_map.svg/330px-NEC_map.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:NEC_map.svg) Sections owned by Amtrak are in red; sections with commuter service are highlighted in blue.

Most of what is now called the Northeast Corridor was built, piece by piece, by several railroads constructed as early as the 1830s. Before 1900, their routes had been consolidated as two long and unconnected stretches, each of them a part of a major railroad. Anchored in Washington, D.C., the stretch owned by the [Pennsylvania Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Railroad "Pennsylvania Railroad"), approached New York City from the south. Anchored at Boston, the stretch owned by the [New Haven Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York,_New_Haven_and_Hartford_Railroad "New York, New Haven and Hartford Railroad") entered [New York State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_State "New York State") from [Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut "Connecticut"). The former terminated at New Jersey [ferry slips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exchange_Place_station_(Pennsylvania_Railroad) "Exchange Place station (Pennsylvania Railroad)") across the Hudson River from Manhattan Island.[&#8202;](#ref-back-9-0)[\[8\]](#refnote-9) The latter extended to the Bronx, where it continued into Manhattan via trackage rights on the [New York and Harlem Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_and_Harlem_Railroad "New York and Harlem Railroad"). It also reached the Bronx via the [Harlem River and Port Chester Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem_River_and_Port_Chester_Railroad "Harlem River and Port Chester Railroad"), which extended to the Bronx from the New Haven at [New Rochelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Rochelle,_New_York "New Rochelle, New York").[&#8202;](#ref-back-10-0)[\[9\]](#refnote-10)

From 1903 to 1917, the two railroads undertook a number of projects that connected their lines and completed, in effect, the Northeast Corridor. These included the [New York Tunnel Extension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Tunnel_Extension "New York Tunnel Extension"), which extended from New Jersey to Long Island (and was composed of the [Manhattan Transfer station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Transfer_station "Manhattan Transfer station"), the [North River Tunnels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_River_Tunnels "North River Tunnels"), a new [Pennsylvania Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(1910%E2%80%931963) "Pennsylvania Station (1910–1963)"), the [East River Tunnels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_River_Tunnels "East River Tunnels")), the [New York Connecting Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Connecting_Railroad "New York Connecting Railroad"), and the [Hell Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hell_Gate_Bridge "Hell Gate Bridge"). Combined, these constituted a stretch that started just outside of [Newark, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey "Newark, New Jersey"), on the Pennsylvania Railroad side, and connected with the [Harlem River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem_River "Harlem River") and Port Chester Railroad (and thus New Rochelle) on the New Haven side. With the opening of the Hell Gate Bridge in 1917, this final connecting stretch, and thus the Northeast Corridor itself, was complete.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

With the 1968 creation of [Penn Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_Central_Transportation_Company "Penn Central Transportation Company"), which was a combination of those two railroads and the [New York Central Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Central_Railroad "New York Central Railroad"), the entire corridor was under the control of a single entity for the first time.[&#8202;](#ref-back-11-0)[\[10\]](#refnote-11) After successor Penn Central’s 1970 bankruptcy, the corridor was almost entirely subsumed by the subsequently-created [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak "Amtrak") through the
Regional Rail Reorganization Act of 1973 and the Railroad Revitalization
and Regulatory Reform Act of 1976,[&#8202;](#ref-back-12-0)[\[11\]](#refnote-12) although Amtrak ran trains as early as 1971.[&#8202;](#ref-back-13-0)[\[12\]](#refnote-13)

#### Boston–The Bronx (New Haven Railroad)

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: Boston–The Bronx (New Haven Railroad)")\]

- [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston "Boston")– [Providence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence,_Rhode_Island "Providence, Rhode Island"): [Boston and Providence Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_and_Providence_Railroad "Boston and Providence Railroad") opened 1835, partially realigned in 1847 and in 1899. Became part of the [Old Colony Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Colony_Railroad "Old Colony Railroad") in 1888.[&#8202;](#ref-back-14-0)[\[13\]](#refnote-14)
- Providence– [Stonington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonington,_Connecticut "Stonington, Connecticut"): [New York, Providence and Boston Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York,_Providence_and_Boston_Railroad "New York, Providence and Boston Railroad") opened 1837; partially realigned 1848.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Stonington– [New Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut "New Haven, Connecticut"): [New Haven, New London and Stonington Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shore_Line_Railway_(Connecticut) "Shore Line Railway (Connecticut)") opened 1852–1889, realigned in New Haven, 1894.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- New Haven– [New Rochelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Rochelle,_New_York "New Rochelle, New York"): [New York and New Haven Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_and_New_Haven_Railroad "New York and New Haven Railroad") opened 1849.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- New Rochelle– [Port Morris (Bronx)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Morris,_Bronx "Port Morris, Bronx"): [Harlem River and Port Chester Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem_River_and_Port_Chester_Railroad "Harlem River and Port Chester Railroad") opened 1873.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

#### Newark–Washington, D.C. (Pennsylvania Railroad)

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Newark–Washington, D.C. (Pennsylvania Railroad)")\]

- [Newark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newark,_New_Jersey "Newark, New Jersey")– [Trenton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey"): [United New Jersey Railroad and Canal Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_New_Jersey_Railroad_and_Canal_Company "United New Jersey Railroad and Canal Company") opened 1834–1839, 1841; partially realigned 1863 and 1870.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Trenton– [Frankford Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankford_Junction "Frankford Junction"): [Philadelphia and Trenton Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_and_Trenton_Railroad "Philadelphia and Trenton Railroad") opened 1834; partially realigned 1911.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Frankford Junction–Zoo Tower: [Connecting Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecting_Railway "Connecting Railway") opened 1867.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Zoo Tower– [Grays Ferry Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gray's_Ferry_Bridge "Gray's Ferry Bridge"): [Junction Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junction_Railroad_(Philadelphia) "Junction Railroad (Philadelphia)") opened 1863–1866.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Grays Ferry–Bayview: [Philadelphia, Wilmington and Baltimore Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia,_Wilmington_and_Baltimore_Railroad "Philadelphia, Wilmington and Baltimore Railroad") opened 1837–1838,[&#8202;](#ref-back-15-0)[\[14\]](#refnote-15) 1866, 1906.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]
- Bayview Yard– [Baltimore Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(Baltimore) "Pennsylvania Station (Baltimore)"): [Union Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Tunnel_(Baltimore) "Union Tunnel (Baltimore)") opened 1873.[&#8202;](#ref-back-16-0)[\[15\]](#refnote-16)
- [Baltimore Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(Baltimore) "Pennsylvania Station (Baltimore)")– [Landover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landover,_Maryland "Landover, Maryland"): [Baltimore and Potomac Rail Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_and_Potomac_Rail_Road "Baltimore and Potomac Rail Road") opened 1872.[&#8202;](#ref-back-17-0)[\[16\]](#refnote-17)
- Landover– [Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D.C. "Washington, D.C."): [Magruder Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_and_Potomac_Railroad#New_Washington_Union_Station_alignment "Baltimore and Potomac Railroad") opened 1907[&#8202;](#ref-back-18-0)[\[17\]](#refnote-18)

### Electrification, 1905–38

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Electrification, 1905–38")\]

#### New York section

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: New York section")\]

See also: [Electrification of the New York, New Haven, and Hartford Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrification_of_the_New_York,_New_Haven,_and_Hartford_Railroad "Electrification of the New York, New Haven, and Hartford Railroad")

In 1899, [William J. Wilgus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_J._Wilgus "William J. Wilgus"), the New York Central Railroad (NYC)'s chief engineer, proposed [electrifying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railway_electrification_system "Railway electrification system") the lines leading from [Grand Central Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Central_Terminal "Grand Central Terminal") and the split at [Mott Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mott_Haven,_Bronx "Mott Haven, Bronx"), using a [third rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_rail "Third rail") power system devised by [Frank J. Sprague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_J._Sprague "Frank J. Sprague"). Electricity was in use on some branch lines of the NYNH&H for [interurban streetcars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interurban "Interurban") via [third rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_rail "Third rail") or [trolley wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overhead_line "Overhead line").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sprague_Cunningham_2013_pp._58–76-0)[\[20\]](#refnote-Sprague_Cunningham_2013_pp._58–76) An accident in the [Park Avenue Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Avenue_Tunnel_(railroad) "Park Avenue Tunnel (railroad)") near the present Grand Central Terminal that killed 17 people on January 8, 1902, was blamed on smoke from [steam locomotives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steam_locomotive "Steam locomotive"); the resulting outcry led to a push for electric operation in [Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan "Manhattan").[&#8202;](#ref-back-nyt-2013-0)[\[21\]](#refnote-nyt-2013-01)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Roberts2013-0)[\[22\]](#refnote-Roberts2013)[&#8202;](#ref-back-24-0)[\[23\]](#refnote-24)

The NH announced in 1905 that it would electrify its main line from New York to [Stamford, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamford,_Connecticut "Stamford, Connecticut").\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Along with the [construction of Grand Central Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Construction_of_Grand_Central_Terminal "Construction of Grand Central Terminal"), which was opened in 1913, the NYC electrified its lines. On September 30, 1906, the NYC conducted a test of suburban [multiple unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_unit "Multiple unit") service to [High Bridge station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Bridge_Station_(New_York) "High Bridge Station (New York)") on the [Hudson Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hudson_Line_(Metro-North) "Hudson Line (Metro-North)");[&#8202;](#ref-back-Schlichting2001-0)[\[24\]](#refnote-Schlichting2001):97[&#8202;](#ref-back-26-0)[\[25\]](#refnote-26) regular service began on December 11.[&#8202;](#ref-back-27-0)[\[26\]](#refnote-27)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Burch_1911_p._541-0)[\[27\]](#refnote-Burch_1911_p._541) [Electric locomotives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_locomotive "Electric locomotive") began serving Grand Central on February 15, 1907,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Schlichting2001-1)[\[24\]](#refnote-Schlichting2001):115 and all NYC passenger service into Grand Central was electrified on July 1, 1907.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Burch_1911_p._541-1)[\[27\]](#refnote-Burch_1911_p._541)[&#8202;](#ref-back-29-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-29) NH electrification began in July to [New Rochelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Rochelle,_New_York "New Rochelle, New York"), August to [Port Chester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Chester,_New_York "Port Chester, New York") and October the rest of the way to Stamford.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Goss_1915_p._635-0)[\[29\]](#refnote-Goss_1915_p._635) Steam trains last operated into Grand Central on June 30, 1908: the deadline after which steam trains were banned in Manhattan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Schlichting2001-2)[\[24\]](#refnote-Schlichting2001):55–56 Subsequently, all NH passenger trains into Manhattan were electrified. In June 1914, the NH electrification was extended to [New Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut "New Haven, Connecticut"), which was the terminus of electrified service for over 80 years.[&#8202;](#ref-back-31-0)[\[30\]](#refnote-31)

The PRR was building its [Pennsylvania Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(New_York_City) "Pennsylvania Station (New York City)") and electrified approaches, which were served by the PRR's lines in [New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey "New Jersey") and the [Long Island Rail Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island_Rail_Road "Long Island Rail Road") (LIRR). LIRR electric service began in 1905 on the [Atlantic Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Branch "Atlantic Branch") from [downtown Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Brooklyn "Downtown Brooklyn") past [Jamaica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaica_(LIRR_station) "Jamaica (LIRR station)"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ziel_2013_p._108-0)[\[31\]](#refnote-Ziel_2013_p._108)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Keller_Lynch_2005_p._14-0)[\[32\]](#refnote-Keller_Lynch_2005_p._14) and in June 1910 on the branch to [Long Island City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island_City "Long Island City"): part of the main line to Penn Station.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Keller_Lynch_2005_p._14-1)[\[32\]](#refnote-Keller_Lynch_2005_p._14) Penn Station opened on September 8, 1910, for LIRR trains[&#8202;](#ref-back-34-0)[\[33\]](#refnote-34) and November 27 for the PRR;[&#8202;](#ref-back-35-0)[\[34\]](#refnote-35) trains of both railroads were powered by [DC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_current "Direct current") electricity from a third rail. PRR trains changed engines (electric to/from steam) at [Manhattan Transfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan_Transfer_(PRR_station) "Manhattan Transfer (PRR station)"); passengers could also transfer there to [H&M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Authority_Trans-Hudson "Port Authority Trans-Hudson") trains to downtown Manhattan.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

On July 29, 1911, NH began electric service on its [Harlem River Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harlem_River_Branch "Harlem River Branch"): a suburban branch that would become a main line with the completion of the [New York Connecting Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Connecting_Railroad "New York Connecting Railroad") and its [Hell Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hell_Gate_Bridge "Hell Gate Bridge").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Electric_Railway_Journal_1912_p._893-0)[\[35\]](#refnote-Electric_Railway_Journal_1912_p._893)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Report_1911_p._1-PA9-0)[\[36\]](#refnote-Report_1911_p._1-PA9) The bridge opened on March 9, 1917,[&#8202;](#ref-back-nrhs1917-2)[\[19\]](#refnote-nrhs1917) but was operated by steam with an engine change at [Sunnyside Yard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunnyside_Yard "Sunnyside Yard") east of Penn Station until 1918.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Further information: [Amtrak's 60 Hz traction power system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak's_60_Hz_traction_power_system "Amtrak's 60 Hz traction power system")

Electrification north of New Haven to Providence and Boston had been planned by the NH, and authorized by the company's board of directors shortly before the United States entered [World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_I "World War I"). This plan was not carried out because of the war and the company's financial problems. Electrification north of New Haven did not occur until the 1990s, by Amtrak, using a 60Hz system.

#### New York to Washington electrification

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: New York to Washington electrification")\]

Main article: [Amtrak's 25 Hz traction power system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak's_25_Hz_traction_power_system "Amtrak's 25 Hz traction power system")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/K_Tower_Amtrak_2008b.jpg/250px-K_Tower_Amtrak_2008b.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:K_Tower_Amtrak_2008b.jpg)"K" Tower, north of [Washington Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Union_Station "Washington Union Station"), is the only remaining [interlocking tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interlocking_tower "Interlocking tower") on the Northeast Corridor south of Philadelphia

In 1905, the PRR began to electrify its suburban lines at Philadelphia, an effort that eventually led to [11 kV, 25 Hz AC catenary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak's_25_Hz_Traction_Power_System "Amtrak's 25 Hz Traction Power System") from New York and Washington.[&#8202;](#ref-back-PRR_Electrification-0)[\[37\]](#refnote-PRR_Electrification) Electric service began in September 1915, with [multiple unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_unit "Multiple unit") trains west to [Paoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paoli,_Pennsylvania "Paoli, Pennsylvania") on the PRR Main Line (now the [Keystone Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keystone_Corridor "Keystone Corridor")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-39-0)[\[38\]](#refnote-39) Electric service to [Chestnut Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chestnut_Hill_(PA) "Chestnut Hill (PA)") (now the [Chestnut Hill West Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chestnut_Hill_West_Line "Chestnut Hill West Line")), including a stretch of the NEC, began on March 30, 1918.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Local electric service to [Wilmington, Delaware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmington,_Delaware "Wilmington, Delaware"), on the NEC began on September 30, 1928, and to [Trenton, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey"), on June 29, 1930.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

Electrified service between [Exchange Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exchange_Place_(PRR_station) "Exchange Place (PRR station)"), the [Jersey City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_City,_New_Jersey "Jersey City, New Jersey") terminal, and [New Brunswick, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey "New Brunswick, New Jersey"), began on December 8, 1932, including the extension of Penn Station electric service from Manhattan Transfer.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] On January 16, 1933, the rest of the electrification between New Brunswick and Trenton opened, giving a fully-electrified line between New York and Wilmington. Trains to Washington began running under electricity to Wilmington on February 12, 1933, with the engine-change moved from Manhattan Transfer to Wilmington.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] The same was done on April 9, 1933, for trains running west from Philadelphia, with the change point moved to Paoli.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

In 1933, the electrification south of Wilmington was stalled by the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression "Great Depression"), but the PRR got a loan from the [Public Works Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Works_Administration "Public Works Administration") to resume work.[&#8202;](#ref-back-40-0)[\[39\]](#refnote-40) The tunnels at Baltimore were rebuilt as part of the project. Electric service between New York and Washington began on February 10, 1935.[&#8202;](#ref-back-41-0)[\[40\]](#refnote-41) On April 7, the electrification of passenger trains was complete, with 639 daily trains: 191 hauled by locomotives and the other 448 under multiple-unit power.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] New York–Washington electric freight service began on May 20, 1935, after the electrification of freight lines in New Jersey and Washington, DC. \[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Extensions to [Potomac Yard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potomac_Yard "Potomac Yard") across the [Potomac River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potomac_River "Potomac River") from Washington, as well as several freight branches along the way, were electrified in 1937 and 1938.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] The Potomac Yard retained its electrification until 1981.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

#### Re-signaling

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Re-signaling")\]

In the 1930s, PRR equipped the New York–Washington line with [Pulse code cab signaling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulse_code_cab_signaling "Pulse code cab signaling"). Between 1998 and 2003, this system was overlaid with an [Alstom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alstom "Alstom") [Advanced Civil Speed Enforcement System (ACSES)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_Civil_Speed_Enforcement_System "Advanced Civil Speed Enforcement System"), using track-mounted transponders similar to the [Balises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balise "Balise") of the modern [European Train Control System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Train_Control_System "European Train Control System").[&#8202;](#ref-back-42-0)[\[41\]](#refnote-42) The ACSES enabled Amtrak to implement [positive train control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Positive_train_control "Positive train control") to comply with the [Rail Safety Improvement Act of 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_Safety_Improvement_Act_of_2008 "Rail Safety Improvement Act of 2008").\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

### Founding and operation of Amtrak

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Founding and operation of Amtrak")\]

#### Reorganization and bankruptcy

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Reorganization and bankruptcy")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/The_Congressional_Pennsylvania_Railroad.JPG/250px-The_Congressional_Pennsylvania_Railroad.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:The_Congressional_Pennsylvania_Railroad.JPG) Pennsylvania Railroad's _Congressional_ west of the [North River Tunnels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_River_Tunnels "North River Tunnels") on its way to [Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington,_D.C. "Washington, D.C.")

In December 1967, the [UAC TurboTrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAC_TurboTrain "UAC TurboTrain") set a speed record for a production train: 170.8 miles per hour (274.9km/h) between [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey "New Brunswick, New Jersey") and [Trenton, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey").[&#8202;](#ref-back-43-0)[\[42\]](#refnote-43)

In February 1968, PRR merged with its rival [New York Central Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Central_Railroad "New York Central Railroad") to form the [Penn Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_Central_Transportation_Company "Penn Central Transportation Company") (PC).[&#8202;](#ref-back-44-0)[\[43\]](#refnote-44) Penn Central was required to absorb the New Haven in 1969 as a condition of the merger.[&#8202;](#ref-back-45-0)[\[44\]](#refnote-45)

On September 21, 1970, all New York–Boston trains except the _Turboservice_ were rerouted into Penn Station from Grand Central;\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] the _Turboservice_ moved on February 1, 1971, for cross-platform transfers to the Metroliners.[&#8202;](#ref-back-penn_chronology-0)[\[45\]](#refnote-penn_chronology)

In 1971, Amtrak began operations, and various state governments took control of portions of the NEC for their commuter transportation authorities. In January, the [Commonwealth of Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts "Massachusetts") bought the [Attleboro/Stoughton Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence/Stoughton_Line "Providence/Stoughton Line") in Massachusetts,\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] later operated by the [Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massachusetts_Bay_Transportation_Authority "Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority"). The same month, the New York State Metropolitan Transportation Authority bought, and Connecticut leased, from Penn Central their sections of the [New Haven Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven_Line "New Haven Line"), between [Woodlawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodlawn,_Bronx "Woodlawn, Bronx"), New York, and [New Haven, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven,_Connecticut "New Haven, Connecticut").[&#8202;](#ref-back-penn_chronology-1)[\[45\]](#refnote-penn_chronology)

In 1973, the [Regional Rail Reorganization Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Railway_Association "United States Railway Association") opened the way for Amtrak to buy sections of the NEC not already been sold to these commuter transportation authorities. These purchases by Amtrak were controversial at the time, and the [Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Transportation "United States Department of Transportation") blocked the transaction and withheld purchase funds for several months until Amtrak granted it control over reconstruction of the corridor.[&#8202;](#ref-back-47-0)[\[46\]](#refnote-47)

In February 1975, the Preliminary System Plan for [Conrail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail "Conrail") proposed to stop running freight trains on the NEC between [Groton, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groton,_Connecticut "Groton, Connecticut"), and [Hillsgrove, Rhode Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillsgrove,_Rhode_Island "Hillsgrove, Rhode Island"), but this clause was rejected the following month by the [U.S. Railway Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Railway_Association "United States Railway Association").[&#8202;](#ref-back-48-0)[\[47\]](#refnote-48)

By April 1976, Amtrak owned the entire NEC except Boston to the RI state line, which is owned by the Commonwealth of Massachusetts, and New Haven to New Rochelle, New York, which is owned by the States of Connecticut and New York. Amtrak still operates and maintains the portion in Massachusetts, but the line from New Haven to [New Rochelle, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Rochelle,_New_York "New Rochelle, New York"), is operated by the [Metro-North Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro-North_Railroad "Metro-North Railroad"), which has hindered the establishment of high-speed service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-49-0)[\[48\]](#refnote-49)[&#8202;](#ref-back-50-0)[\[49\]](#refnote-50)

#### Northeast Corridor Improvement Project

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Northeast Corridor Improvement Project")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Northeast_Corridor_Improvement_Project_track_work%2C_April_1979.jpg/250px-Northeast_Corridor_Improvement_Project_track_work%2C_April_1979.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Northeast_Corridor_Improvement_Project_track_work,_April_1979.jpg) Northeast Corridor Improvement Project track work in April 1979

In 1976, [Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Congress "United States Congress") authorized an overhaul of the system between Washington and Boston.[&#8202;](#ref-back-4R_Act-0)[\[50\]](#refnote-4R_Act) Called the Northeast Corridor Improvement Project (NECIP), it included safety improvements, modernization of the signaling system by [General Railway Signal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Railway_Signal "General Railway Signal"), and new Centralized Electrification and Traffic Control (CETC) control centers by [Chrysler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrysler "Chrysler") at Philadelphia, New York and Boston.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] It allowed more trains to run faster and closer together, and set the stage for later [high-speed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail "High-speed rail") operation. NECIP also introduced the [AEM-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AEM-7 "AEM-7") locomotive, which lowered travel times and became the most successful engine on the Corridor. The NECIP set travel time goals of 2 hours and 40 minutes between Washington and New York, and 3 hours and 40 minutes between Boston and New York.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Redirection_Study-0)[\[51\]](#refnote-Redirection_Study) These goals were not met because of the low level of funding provided by the Reagan Administration and Congress in the 1980s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Master_Plan-0)[\[52\]](#refnote-Master_Plan)

Electrification between New Haven and Boston was to be included in the 1976 [Railroad Revitalization and Regulatory Reform Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railroad_Revitalization_and_Regulatory_Reform_Act "Railroad Revitalization and Regulatory Reform Act").[&#8202;](#ref-back-4R_Act-1)[\[50\]](#refnote-4R_Act)

The last [grade crossings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Level_crossing "Level crossing") between New York and Washington were closed about 1985; eleven grade crossings remain in Connecticut.

#### 1990s implementation of high-speed rail

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: 1990s implementation of high-speed rail")\]

Main article: [Amtrak 60 Hz traction power system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_60_Hz_traction_power_system "Amtrak 60 Hz traction power system")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Southbound_Acela_Express_crossing_the_Susquehanna_River_Bridge.jpg/250px-Southbound_Acela_Express_crossing_the_Susquehanna_River_Bridge.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Southbound_Acela_Express_crossing_the_Susquehanna_River_Bridge.jpg) Amtrak _[Acela Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela_Express "Acela Express")_ crosses the [Susquehanna River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susquehanna_River "Susquehanna River") in Maryland on a [bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Susquehanna_River_Bridge "Amtrak Susquehanna River Bridge") built by the PRR in 1906.

In the 1990s, Amtrak upgraded the NEC north of New Haven, CT to get it ready for the high-speed _[Acela Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela_Express "Acela Express")_ trains.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Master_Plan-1)[\[52\]](#refnote-Master_Plan) Dubbed the Northeast High Speed Rail Improvement Program (NHRIP), the effort eliminated [grade crossings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Level_crossing "Level crossing"), rebuilt bridges and modified curves. Concrete [railroad ties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railroad_tie "Railroad tie") replaced wood ties, and heavier [continuous welded rail (CWR)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Track_(rail_transport)#Continuous_welded_rail "Track (rail transport)") was laid-down.[&#8202;](#ref-back-54-0)[\[53\]](#refnote-54)[&#8202;](#ref-back-55-0)[\[54\]](#refnote-55) In 1996, Amtrak began installing electrification infrastructure along the 157 miles (253 kilometres) of track between New Haven and Boston.

#### 2000–present

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: 2000–present")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/2023-10-05_10_32_46_View_southwest_along_the_Northeast_Corridor_rail_line_from_the_overpass_over_Mercer_County_Route_614_%28Nottingham_Way%29_in_Hamilton_Township%2C_Mercer_County%2C_New_Jersey.jpg/250px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2023-10-05_10_32_46_View_southwest_along_the_Northeast_Corridor_rail_line_from_the_overpass_over_Mercer_County_Route_614_(Nottingham_Way)_in_Hamilton_Township,_Mercer_County,_New_Jersey.jpg) View along the Northeast Corridor tracks between Hamilton and Trenton in central New Jersey

Service with electric locomotives between New Haven and Boston began on January 31, 2000.[&#8202;](#ref-back-56-0)[\[55\]](#refnote-56) The project took four years and cost close to $2.3 billion: $1.3 billion for the infrastructure improvements and close to $1 billion for both the new [Acela Express trainsets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela_Express_(trainset) "Acela Express (trainset)") and the [Bombardier–Alstom HHP-8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier%E2%80%93Alstom_HHP-8 "Bombardier–Alstom HHP-8") locomotives.[&#8202;](#ref-back-57-0)[\[56\]](#refnote-57)

On December 11, 2000, Amtrak began operating its higher-speed _Acela Express_ service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-58-0)[\[57\]](#refnote-58) Fastest travel time by _Acela_ is three and a half hours between Boston and New York, and two hours forty-five minutes between New York and Washington, D.C.[&#8202;](#ref-back-59-0)[\[58\]](#refnote-59)

In 2005, there was talk in Congress of splitting the Northeast Corridor, which was opposed by then-acting Amtrak president [David Gunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_L._Gunn "David L. Gunn"). The plan, supported by the Bush administration, would "turn over the Northeast Corridor – the tracks from Washington to Boston that are the railroad's main physical asset – to a federal-state consortium."[&#8202;](#ref-back-anytfiring-0)[\[59\]](#refnote-anytfiring)

With the passage of the [Passenger Rail Investment and Improvement Act of 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passenger_Rail_Investment_and_Improvement_Act_of_2008 "Passenger Rail Investment and Improvement Act of 2008"), the Congress established the [Northeast Corridor Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Corridor_Commission "Northeast Corridor Commission") (NEC Commission) in the U.S. Department of Transportation to facilitate mutual cooperation and planning and to advise Congress on Corridor rail and development policy. The commission members include USDOT, Amtrak and the Northeast Corridor states.

In October 2010, Amtrak released "A Vision for High-Speed Rail on the Northeast Corridor," an aspirational proposal for dedicated high-speed rail tracks between Washington, D.C., and Boston.[&#8202;](#ref-back-61-0)[\[60\]](#refnote-61) Many of these proposals are unfunded.

In August 2011 the [United States Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Transportation "United States Department of Transportation") committed $450 million to a six-year project to support capacity increases on one of the busiest segments on the NEC: a 24-mile (39km) section between [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey "New Brunswick, New Jersey") and [Trenton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey"), passing through Princeton Junction. The [Next Generation High-Speed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail_in_the_United_States#Northeast_Corridor "High-speed rail in the United States") project is designed to upgrade electrical power, signal systems and overhead catenary wires to improve reliability and increase speeds up to 160mph (260km/h), and, after the purchase of new equipment, up to 186 miles per hour (299km/h).[&#8202;](#ref-back-obligated-0)[\[61\]](#refnote-obligated) In September 2012, speed tests were conducted using _Acela_ trainsets, achieving a speed of 165 miles per hour (266km/h).[&#8202;](#ref-back-63-0)[\[62\]](#refnote-63)[&#8202;](#ref-back-64-0)[\[63\]](#refnote-64) The improvements were scheduled to be completed in 2016, but, due to delays, the project had not been completed until 2020.[&#8202;](#ref-back-65-0)[\[64\]](#refnote-65)[&#8202;](#ref-back-66-0)[\[65\]](#refnote-66)

In 2012, the [Federal Railroad Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Railroad_Administration "Federal Railroad Administration") began developing a master plan for bringing high-speed rail to the Northeast Corridor titled NEC FUTURE, and released the final [environmental impact statement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_statement "Environmental impact statement") in December 2016.[&#8202;](#ref-back-67-0)[\[66\]](#refnote-67) Multiple potential alignments north of New York City were studied.[&#8202;](#ref-back-68-0)[\[67\]](#refnote-68) The proposed upgrades have not been funded.

##### 2015 derailment

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: 2015 derailment")\]

Main article: [2015 Philadelphia train derailment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Philadelphia_train_derailment "2015 Philadelphia train derailment")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/NTSB_2015_Philadelphia_train_derailment_3.jpg/250px-NTSB_2015_Philadelphia_train_derailment_3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:NTSB_2015_Philadelphia_train_derailment_3.jpg) [NTSB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NTSB "NTSB") officials inspect the derailed locomotive 601

Eleven minutes after leaving [30th Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30th_Street_Station "30th Street Station") in Philadelphia on May 12, 2015, a year-old [ACS-64](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siemens_ACS-64 "Siemens ACS-64") locomotive (#601) and all seven [Amfleet I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amfleet "Amfleet") coaches of Amtrak's northbound _[Northeast Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Regional "Northeast Regional")_ (TR#188) derailed at 9:21pm at [Frankford Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankford_Junction "Frankford Junction") in the [Port Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Richmond,_Philadelphia "Port Richmond, Philadelphia") section of the city, while entering a 50mph (80km/h) speed limited (but at the time non- [ATC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automatic_train_control "Automatic train control") protected) [4°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Degree_of_curvature "Degree of curvature") curve at 106mph (171km/h), killing eight and injuring more than 200 (eight critically) of the 238 passengers and five crew on board as well as causing the suspension of all Philadelphia–New York NEC service for six days.[&#8202;](#ref-back-69-0)[\[68\]](#refnote-69)[&#8202;](#ref-back-70-0)[\[69\]](#refnote-70)

The deadliest crash on the Northeast Corridor was the 1987 Maryland train collision, in which 16 people died when Amtrak's Washington–Boston _Colonial_ (TR#94) [rear-ended](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Maryland_train_collision "1987 Maryland train collision") three stationary [Conrail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail "Conrail") locomotives at Gunpow Interlocking near Baltimore on January 4, 1987.[&#8202;](#ref-back-71-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-71) Frankford Junction curve was the site of a previous [fatal accident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1943_Frankford_Junction_train_wreck "1943 Frankford Junction train wreck") on September 6, 1943, when an extra section of the [PRR's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Railroad "Pennsylvania Railroad") Washington to New York _Congressional Limited_ derailed there, killing 79 and injuring 117 of the 541 on board.[&#8202;](#ref-back-72-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-72)

## Infrastructure

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Infrastructure")\]

Main article: [List of Northeast Corridor infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Northeast_Corridor_infrastructure "List of Northeast Corridor infrastructure")

The NEC is a cooperative venture between Amtrak and various state agencies. Amtrak owns the track between Washington and [New Rochelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Rochelle,_New_York "New Rochelle, New York"), [New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_(state) "New York (state)"), a northern suburb of New York City.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] The segment from New Rochelle to New Haven is owned by the states of New York and Connecticut; [Metro-North Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro-North_Railroad "Metro-North Railroad") commuter trains operate there.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] Amtrak owns the tracks north of New Haven to the border between Rhode Island and Massachusetts. The final segment from the border north to Boston is owned by the Commonwealth of Massachusetts.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

### Electrification

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Electrification")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Amtrak_constant_tension_catenary_gantry_and_weights_near_Readville_September_2025.jpg/250px-Amtrak_constant_tension_catenary_gantry_and_weights_near_Readville_September_2025.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Amtrak_constant_tension_catenary_gantry_and_weights_near_Readville_September_2025.jpg) Constant-tension catenary on Amtrak's 60 Hz system[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Wires%2C_Wires_Everywhere_%286468558851%29.jpg/250px-Wires%2C_Wires_Everywhere_%286468558851%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Wires,_Wires_Everywhere_(6468558851).jpg) Older variable-tension catenary in Perryville, MD, on the ex-Pennsylvania Railroad 25Hz system

At just over 453 miles (729km), the Northeast Corridor is the longest electrified rail corridor in the United States.[&#8202;](#ref-back-73-0)[\[72\]](#refnote-73)\[ _[user-generated source?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:UGC "Wikipedia:UGC")_\]

Currently, the corridor uses three catenary systems. From Washington, D.C., to Sunnyside Yard (just east of New York Penn Station), [Amtrak's 25 Hz traction power system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak's_25_Hz_traction_power_system "Amtrak's 25 Hz traction power system") (originally built by the Pennsylvania Railroad) supplies 12kV at 25Hz. From Sunnyside to Mill River (just east of New Haven station), the [former New Haven Railroad's system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrification_of_the_New_York,_New_Haven,_and_Hartford_Railroad "Electrification of the New York, New Haven, and Hartford Railroad"), since modified by Metro-North, supplies 12.5kV at 60Hz. From Mill River to Boston, the much newer [60 Hz traction power system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak's_60_Hz_traction_power_system "Amtrak's 60 Hz traction power system") supplies 25kV at 60Hz. All of Amtrak's electric locomotives can [switch between these systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-system_(rail) "Multi-system (rail)").

[Constant-tension catenary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overhead_line#Tensioning "Overhead line") is used between Pelham, NY and Boston, MA, as well as for a short section in New Jersey. Most of the catenary south of New York City is not constant tension. Trains operating under non-constant-tension catenary are limited to 135 miles per hour (217km/h) to prevent catenary damage, despite _Acela Express_ trains otherwise being capable of higher speeds. Speeds may be further reduced during hot weather due to the non-constant-tension catenary sagging, often causing delays.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Curbed20240709-0)[\[73\]](#refnote-Curbed20240709)

In addition to catenary, the [East River Tunnels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_River_Tunnels "East River Tunnels") have 750 V DC [third rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_rail "Third rail") for [Long Island Rail Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island_Rail_Road "Long Island Rail Road") trains, and the [North River Tunnels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_River_Tunnels "North River Tunnels") have third rail for emergency use only.

In 2006, several high-profile electric-power failures delayed Amtrak and commuter trains on the Northeast Corridor up to five hours.[&#8202;](#ref-back-75-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-75) Railroad officials blamed Amtrak's funding woes for the deterioration of the track and power supply system, which in places is almost a hundred years old. These problems have decreased in recent years after tracks and power systems were repaired and improved.[&#8202;](#ref-back-76-0)[\[75\]](#refnote-76)

In September 2013, one of two feeder lines supplying power to the [New Haven Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven_Line "New Haven Line") failed, while the other feeder was disabled for service. The lack of electrical power disrupted trains on Amtrak and [Metro-North Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro-North_Railroad "Metro-North Railroad"), which share the segment in New York State.[&#8202;](#ref-back-77-0)[\[76\]](#refnote-77)

Numerous catenary and electrical issues slowed and interrupted service in New York and New Jersey during the spring and especially summer of 2024, leading to the latter being dubbed the "summer of hell".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Curbed20240709-1)[\[73\]](#refnote-Curbed20240709)[&#8202;](#ref-back-78-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-78) After Amtrak spent $27 million on infrastructure repairs, service improved in 2025 with significantly fewer cancelled New Jersey Transit trains.[&#8202;](#ref-back-79-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-79)

### Stations

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Stations")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/30th_Street_Station_Philadelphia_July_2016_002_edit.jpg/250px-30th_Street_Station_Philadelphia_July_2016_002_edit.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:30th_Street_Station_Philadelphia_July_2016_002_edit.jpg) [30th Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30th_Street_Station "30th Street Station") in [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia "Philadelphia"), Amtrak's third busiest train station in the nation, July 2016

Main article: [List of Northeast Corridor infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Northeast_Corridor_infrastructure "List of Northeast Corridor infrastructure")

There are 109 active stations on the Northeast Corridor; 30 are used by Amtrak. All but three ( [Kingston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston_station_(Rhode_Island) "Kingston station (Rhode Island)"), [Westerly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westerly_station "Westerly station"), and [Mystic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mystic_station_(Connecticut) "Mystic station (Connecticut)")) see commuter service. Amtrak owns [Pennsylvania Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(New_York_City) "Pennsylvania Station (New York City)") in New York, [30th Street Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/30th_Street_Station "30th Street Station") in Philadelphia, [Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(Baltimore) "Pennsylvania Station (Baltimore)") in Baltimore, and [Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Union_Station "Washington Union Station") in Washington.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\]

The main services of the Northeast Corridor are indicated using the following abbreviations. Other services are listed in the right-most column. Note that not all trains necessarily stop at all indicated stations.

- Amtrak corridor: A ( _[Acela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela "Acela")_), CL ( _[Carolinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolinian_(train) "Carolinian (train)")_), KS ( _[Keystone Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keystone_Service "Keystone Service")_), NR ( _[Northeast Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Regional "Northeast Regional")_), PA ( _[Pennsylvanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvanian_(train) "Pennsylvanian (train)")_), VT ( _[Vermonter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermonter_(train) "Vermonter (train)")_)
- Amtrak long distance: CD ( _[Cardinal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_(train) "Cardinal (train)")_), CS ( _[Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent_(train) "Crescent (train)")_), PL ( _[Palmetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmetto_(train) "Palmetto (train)")_), SM ( _[Silver Meteor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Meteor "Silver Meteor")_)

### Grade crossings

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Grade crossings")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/Passengers_crossing_State_Street.JPG/250px-Passengers_crossing_State_Street.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Passengers_crossing_State_Street.JPG) Passengers crossing the State Street crossing in New London after departing a northbound train[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/Northeast_Regional_at_Miner_Lane_1.JPG/250px-Northeast_Regional_at_Miner_Lane_1.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Northeast_Regional_at_Miner_Lane_1.JPG) A _Northeast Regional_ train crosses Miner Lane in Waterford, the site of a fatal accident in 2005

The entire Northeast Corridor has 11 [grade crossings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Level_crossing "Level crossing"), all in southeastern [New London County, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_London_County,_Connecticut "New London County, Connecticut"). The remaining grade crossings are along a part of the line that hugs the shore of [Long Island Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island_Sound "Long Island Sound"). Some of these crossings constitute the only points of access to waterfront communities and businesses otherwise disconnected from the road network. As such, eliminating them would require [grade separation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grade_separation "Grade separation") to maintain access. Six of the grade crossings have [four-quadrant gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four-quadrant_gate "Four-quadrant gate") with [induction loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Induction_loop "Induction loop") sensors, which allow vehicles stopped on the tracks to be detected in time for an oncoming train to stop. The remaining five grade crossings, 3 near [New London Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_London_Union_Station "New London Union Station") and two in Stonington, have dual gates.[&#8202;](#ref-back-crossings1-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-crossings1)

FRA rules limit track speeds on the corridor to 80 miles per hour (130km/h) over conventional crossings and 95 miles per hour (153km/h) over crossings with four-quadrant gates and vehicle detection tied into the signal system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-81-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-81)

#### History

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: History")\]

The New York to New Haven line has long been completely grade-separated, and the last grade crossings between Washington and New York were eliminated in the 1980s.\[ _[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")_\] In 1994, during planning for electrification and high-speed _[Acela Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela_Express "Acela Express")_ service between New Haven and Boston, a law was passed requiring [USDOT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USDOT "USDOT") to plan for the elimination of all remaining crossings (unless impractical or unnecessary) by 1997.[&#8202;](#ref-back-82-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-82) Some lightly used crossings were simply closed, while most were converted into bridges or underpasses. Only thirteen remained by 1999, of which lightly used crossings in [Old Lyme, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Lyme,_Connecticut "Old Lyme, Connecticut"), and [Exeter, Rhode Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exeter,_Rhode_Island "Exeter, Rhode Island"), were soon closed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-1999issue-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-1999issue)

Despite six nonfatal accidents in the previous sixteen years, there was substantial local opposition to closing the remaining 11 crossings. Outright closing the crossing would eliminate the sole access points to several of the places they served, while grade separation would be expensive and require land takings.[&#8202;](#ref-back-1999issue-1)[\[82\]](#refnote-1999issue) Instead, the crossings were supplied with additional protections. In 1998, School Street in [Groton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groton,_Connecticut "Groton, Connecticut") was the first four-quadrant gate installation in the country with vehicle detection sensors tied into the line's signal system.[&#8202;](#ref-back-84-0)[\[83\]](#refnote-84) It cost $1 million rather than the $4 million for a bridge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-85-0)[\[84\]](#refnote-85) Seven more crossings received similar installations in 1999 and 2000; only the three in New London (which are on a tight curve with speed limits under 30 miles per hour (48km/h)) did not.[&#8202;](#ref-back-86-0)[\[85\]](#refnote-86)

On September 28, 2005, a southbound _Acela Express_ struck a car at Miner Lane in [Waterford, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterford,_Connecticut "Waterford, Connecticut"), the first such incident since the additional protections were implemented.[&#8202;](#ref-back-High-Tech_Gates_Fail_to_Avert_Car-Train_Crash-0)[\[86\]](#refnote-High-Tech_Gates_Fail_to_Avert_Car-Train_Crash) The train was approaching the crossing at approximately 70 miles per hour (110km/h) when the car reportedly rolled under the lowered crossing gate arms too late for the sensor system to fully stop the train. The driver and one passenger were killed on impact; the other passenger died nine days later from injuries sustained in the crash. The gates were later inspected and declared to have been functioning properly at the time of the incident.[&#8202;](#ref-back-88-0)[\[87\]](#refnote-88) The incident drew public criticism about the remaining grade crossings along the busy line.[&#8202;](#ref-back-89-0)[\[88\]](#refnote-89)

## Current rail service

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Current rail service")\]

### Intercity passenger services

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=26 "Edit section: Intercity passenger services")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Amtrak_Crescent_at_Trenton_station%2C_February_2006.jpg/250px-Amtrak_Crescent_at_Trenton_station%2C_February_2006.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Amtrak_Crescent_at_Trenton_station,_February_2006.jpg) New Orleans-bound _[Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent_(train) "Crescent (train)")_ in [Trenton, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey")

In 2003, Amtrak accounted for about 14% of intercity trips between the cities served by the NEC and its branches (the rest were taken by airline, automobile, or [bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercity_bus "Intercity bus")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-100-0)[\[99\]](#refnote-100) A 2011 study estimated that in 2010 Amtrak carried 6% of the Boston–Washington traffic, compared to 80% for automobiles, 8–9% for intercity bus, and 5% for airlines.[&#8202;](#ref-back-otoole20110629-0)[\[100\]](#refnote-otoole20110629) Amtrak's share of the air or rail passenger traffic between New York City and Boston has grown from 20 percent to 54 percent since 2001, and 75 percent between New York City and Washington, D.C.[&#8202;](#ref-back-102-0)[\[101\]](#refnote-102)

These Amtrak trains serve NEC stations and run at least partially on the corridor:

- _[Acela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acela "Acela")_: high-speed rail Boston–Washington, D.C.
- _[Cardinal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_(train) "Cardinal (train)")_: New York–Chicago via Washington, D.C. (Wednesdays, Fridays, and Sundays only)
- _[Carolinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carolinian_(train) "Carolinian (train)")_: New York– [Charlotte, North Carolina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlotte_station_(Amtrak) "Charlotte station (Amtrak)")
- _[Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescent_(train) "Crescent (train)")_: New York– [New Orleans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_Union_Passenger_Terminal "New Orleans Union Passenger Terminal")
- _[Keystone Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keystone_Service "Keystone Service")_: higher-speed rail [Harrisburg, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harrisburg_Transportation_Center "Harrisburg Transportation Center") – New York
- _[Northeast Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Regional "Northeast Regional")_: higher-speed rail (This service qualifies as high speed rail on the 125mph stretch between New Brunswick and Princeton Junction.) Boston/Springfield/New York–Washington D.C./ [Richmond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richmond,_Virginia "Richmond, Virginia")/ [Newport News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newport_News_station "Newport News station")/ [Norfolk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_station_(Amtrak) "Norfolk station (Amtrak)")/ [Roanoke, Virginia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roanoke_station_(Virginia) "Roanoke station (Virginia)")
- _[Palmetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palmetto_(train) "Palmetto (train)")_: [Savannah, Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savannah_station_(Amtrak) "Savannah station (Amtrak)") – New York
- _[Pennsylvanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvanian_(train) "Pennsylvanian (train)")_: [Pittsburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Station_(Pittsburgh) "Union Station (Pittsburgh)")–New York via NEC and [Philadelphia to Harrisburg Main Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia_to_Harrisburg_Main_Line "Philadelphia to Harrisburg Main Line")
- _[Silver Meteor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silver_Meteor "Silver Meteor")_: [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_station_(Amtrak) "Miami station (Amtrak)")–New York
- _[Vermonter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vermonter_(train) "Vermonter (train)")_: [St. Albans, Vermont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Albans_station_(Vermont) "St. Albans station (Vermont)") – Washington, D.C., via NEC and [New Haven–Springfield Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven%E2%80%93Springfield_Line "New Haven–Springfield Line")

Seven other Amtrak trains terminate at NEC stations, but do not use any NEC infrastructure outside the terminus:

- [Amtrak _Hartford Line_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Hartford_Line "Amtrak Hartford Line"): operated in conjunction with [ConnDOT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ConnDOT "ConnDOT"), runs across Amtrak-owned [New Haven–Springfield line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Haven%E2%80%93Springfield_Line "New Haven–Springfield Line") from [Springfield Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield_Union_Station_(Massachusetts) "Springfield Union Station (Massachusetts)") to [New Haven Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Station_(New_Haven) "Union Station (New Haven)"), the latter of which uses NEC infrastructure.

Six Amtrak services operate via the [Empire Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_Corridor "Empire Corridor"), a line largely owned by CSX, with other sections owned by Metro-North Railroad and Amtrak. It meets the NEC at [New York Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(New_York_City) "Pennsylvania Station (New York City)").

- _[Adirondack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adirondack_(train) "Adirondack (train)")_: runs from New York Penn to [Montreal Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montreal_Central_Station "Montreal Central Station")
- _[Berkshire Flyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkshire_Flyer "Berkshire Flyer")_: higher-speed rail from New York Penn to [Albany–Rensselaer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albany%E2%80%93Rensselaer_station "Albany–Rensselaer station") and the [Joseph Scelsi Intermodal Transportation Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Scelsi_Intermodal_Transportation_Center "Joseph Scelsi Intermodal Transportation Center") in [Pittsfield, Massachusetts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsfield,_Massachusetts "Pittsfield, Massachusetts")
- _[Empire Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_Service "Empire Service")_: higher-speed rail from New York Penn to [Albany–Rensselaer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albany%E2%80%93Rensselaer_station "Albany–Rensselaer station") and [Niagara Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niagara_Falls_station_(New_York) "Niagara Falls station (New York)")
- _[Ethan Allen Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethan_Allen_Express "Ethan Allen Express")_: runs from [Burlington Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burlington_Union_Station "Burlington Union Station") to New York Penn
- _[Lake Shore Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Shore_Limited "Lake Shore Limited")_: runs from [Chicago Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station "Chicago Union Station") to New York Penn; also has a branch to the NEC's terminus at [Boston South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Station "South Station")
- _[Maple Leaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maple_Leaf_(train) "Maple Leaf (train)")_: runs from New York Penn to [Toronto Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Station_(Toronto) "Union Station (Toronto)")

The _[Floridian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floridian_(train) "Floridian (train)")_, which travels between [Chicago Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station "Chicago Union Station") and [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_station_(Amtrak) "Miami station (Amtrak)"), intersects with the Northeast Corridor at [Washington Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Union_Station "Washington Union Station").

Due to the wide availability of the _Northeast Regional_, _Keystone Service_, and _Acela_, as well as commuter rail, most long- and medium-haul trains operating along the New York-Washington leg of the NEC do not allow local travel between NEC stations. In most cases, long- and medium-haul trains only stop to discharge passengers from Washington (and in some cases, [Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria,_Virginia "Alexandria, Virginia")) northward, and to receive passengers from Newark to Washington. This policy is intended to keep seats available for passengers making longer trips. The _Vermonter_ and _Palmetto_ are the only medium- and long-haul trains that allow local travel in both directions between New York and Washington. The southbound _Carolinian_ allows local travel daily, while the northbound _Carolinian_ only allows local travel on Sundays, Thursdays, and Fridays. Additionally, the medium-haul _Pennsylvanian_ allows local NEC travel, but this train leaves the corridor in Philadelphia and does not travel all the way to Washington.

### Commuter rail

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=27 "Edit section: Commuter rail")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Trains_at_Ruggles_station%2C_July_2021.jpg/250px-Trains_at_Ruggles_station%2C_July_2021.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Trains_at_Ruggles_station,_July_2021.jpg) Two [MBTA Commuter Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MBTA_Commuter_Rail "MBTA Commuter Rail") trains on the NEC at [Ruggles station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruggles_station "Ruggles station")

In addition to Amtrak, several commuter rail agencies operate passenger service using the NEC tracks:

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority (MBTA) operated by Keolis")\]

- [Providence/Stoughton Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence/Stoughton_Line "Providence/Stoughton Line"): [Wickford Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wickford_Junction_station "Wickford Junction station")–Boston
- [Franklin/Foxboro Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin/Foxboro_Line "Franklin/Foxboro Line"): [Readville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Readville_station "Readville station")–Boston
- [Needham Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Needham_Line "Needham Line"): [Forest Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_Hills_station_(MBTA) "Forest Hills station (MBTA)")–Boston
- [Framingham/Worcester Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Framingham/Worcester_Line "Framingham/Worcester Line"): [Back Bay Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Back_Bay_station "Back Bay station")–Boston

The only section north of New York that does not have commuter service is the 43 miles between Wickford Junction and New London.
