### Freight services

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=35 "Edit section: Freight services")\]

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| [![icon](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **needs [more citations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Northeast_Corridor "Special:EditPage/Northeast Corridor") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners"). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence "Wikipedia:Verifiability")._(November 2022)__([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))_ |

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/NS_Daytime_Freight_on_the_Corridor_%286456921797%29.jpg/250px-NS_Daytime_Freight_on_the_Corridor_%286456921797%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:NS_Daytime_Freight_on_the_Corridor_(6456921797).jpg) [Norfolk Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern_Railway "Norfolk Southern Railway") freight operating on the NEC in [Aberdeen, Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen,_Maryland "Aberdeen, Maryland")

Freight trains operate on parts of the NEC through [trackage rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrangements_between_railroads "Arrangements between railroads"). Prior to the 1970s when [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak "Amtrak") took over all passenger service, the NEC routinely saw lengthy freight trains sometimes numbering over one hundred cars traversing great lengths of the corridor. All freight operations ultimately came under the control of [Penn Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_Central "Penn Central") in the late 1960s and later [Conrail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail "Conrail") upon its formation in 1976, however Amtrak, whose ridership was steadily increasing began demanding heavier taxes for longer trains. Ultimately Conrail began reducing freight service to only small, local trains on certain sections of the corridor where most needed once longer freights began causing congestion and bigger delays with passenger service.

Currently, [Norfolk Southern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norfolk_Southern_Railway "Norfolk Southern Railway") operates over the line south of Philadelphia. [CSX Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CSX_Transportation "CSX Transportation") has rights from New York to New Haven; in Massachusetts; and in [Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maryland "Maryland") from [Landover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landover,_Maryland "Landover, Maryland"), where its [Landover Subdivision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landover_Subdivision "Landover Subdivision") joins the NEC, to [Bowie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bowie,_Maryland "Bowie, Maryland"), where its [Pope's Creek Subdivision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope's_Creek_Subdivision "Pope's Creek Subdivision") leaves it. Between Philadelphia and New York, [Conrail Shared Assets Operations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrail_Shared_Assets_Operations "Conrail Shared Assets Operations") operates as a local switching and terminal company for CSX and Norfolk Southern. The [Providence and Worcester Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence_and_Worcester_Railroad "Providence and Worcester Railroad") operates local freight service from New Haven into Rhode Island and has [overhead trackage rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrangements_between_railroads "Arrangements between railroads") from New Haven to New York over the [Hell Gate Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hell_Gate_Bridge "Hell Gate Bridge") to [Fresh Pond Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresh_Pond_Junction "Fresh Pond Junction").[&#8202;](#ref-back-103-0)[\[102\]](#refnote-103) Additionally, the [Canadian Pacific Kansas City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Pacific_Kansas_City "Canadian Pacific Kansas City") and the [New York and Atlantic Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_and_Atlantic_Railway "New York and Atlantic Railway") both have trackage rights over the Hell Gate Bridge in order to connect with their own routes near New York.[&#8202;](#ref-back-104-0)[\[103\]](#refnote-104)

## Future NEC projects

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=36 "Edit section: Future NEC projects")\]

### Frederick Douglass Tunnel

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Frederick Douglass Tunnel")\]

Main article: [Baltimore and Potomac Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_and_Potomac_Tunnel "Baltimore and Potomac Tunnel")

In January 2010, $60 million was allocated under the [American Recovery and Reinvestment Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Recovery_and_Reinvestment_Act_of_2009 "American Recovery and Reinvestment Act of 2009") to begin studying possible replacements to the [Baltimore and Potomac Tunnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_and_Potomac_Tunnel "Baltimore and Potomac Tunnel"), a major bottleneck on the Northeast Corridor between [Baltimore Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltimore_Penn_Station "Baltimore Penn Station") and Washington, D.C.[&#8202;](#ref-back-105-0)[\[104\]](#refnote-105) Dating back to the 1860s, the design and age of the tunnel limits trains to 30 miles per hour (48km/h), limits the installation of fire and safety systems, and requires costly maintenance to keep operational.[&#8202;](#ref-back-106-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-106) The replacement tunnel is intended to allow speeds up to 100 miles per hour (160km/h) as well as increasing capacity and safety.[&#8202;](#ref-back-107-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-107) After preliminary and final design downselection in 2014 and 2016, respectively, and FRA approval in 2017, the replacement tunnel design was fully finalized in 2021,[&#8202;](#ref-back-108-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-108)[&#8202;](#ref-back-109-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-109)[&#8202;](#ref-back-110-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-110)[&#8202;](#ref-back-111-0)[\[110\]](#refnote-111) After receiving a $4.7 billion grant through the [Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure_Investment_and_Jobs_Act "Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act") in November 2023, the construction contract was awarded to the Kiewit/J.F. Shea joint venture in February 2024 and construction work began later that month, with completion expected in 2035.[&#8202;](#ref-back-112-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-112)[&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-113)[&#8202;](#ref-back-114-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-114)[&#8202;](#ref-back-115-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-115)

### Gateway Program

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=38 "Edit section: Gateway Program")\]

You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=). _(August 2025)_ |

Main article: [Gateway Program (Northeast Corridor)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_Program_(Northeast_Corridor) "Gateway Program (Northeast Corridor)")

In February 2011, Amtrak announced plans for the [Gateway Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_Project "Gateway Project") between [Newark Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(Newark) "Pennsylvania Station (Newark)") and [New York Penn Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Station_(New_York_City) "Pennsylvania Station (New York City)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-GatewayPDF-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-GatewayPDF) The planned project would create a high-speed alignment across the [New Jersey Meadowlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey_Meadowlands "New Jersey Meadowlands") and under the [Hudson River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_River_(Hudson_River) "North River (Hudson River)"), including the replacement of the [Portal Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portal_Bridge "Portal Bridge"), a bottleneck.

### Harold Interlocking

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section: Harold Interlocking")\]

Main article: [Harold Interlocking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harold_Interlocking "Harold Interlocking")

In May 2011, a $294.7-million federal grant was awarded to fix congestion at Harold Interlocking, the USA's second-busiest [rail junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junction_(rail) "Junction (rail)") after [Sunnyside Yard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunnyside_Yard "Sunnyside Yard"). The work will lay tracks to the New York Connecting Railroad [right of way](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-of-way_(transportation) "Right-of-way (transportation)"), allowing Amtrak trains arriving from or bound for [New England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_England "New England") to avoid [NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit "NJ Transit") and [Long Island Rail Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Island_Rail_Road "Long Island Rail Road") trains.[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-117)[&#8202;](#ref-back-118-0)[\[117\]](#refnote-118) Financing for the project was jeopardized in July 2011 by the [House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives "United States House of Representatives"), which voted to divert the funding to unrelated projects.[&#8202;](#ref-back-divertfunds-0)[\[118\]](#refnote-divertfunds) The project was then funded by FRA and the MTA.[&#8202;](#ref-back-120-0)[\[119\]](#refnote-120)As of 2018[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit), the interlocking is being reconstructed for LIRR's [East Side Access](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Side_Access "East Side Access") project.[&#8202;](#ref-back-121-0)[\[120\]](#refnote-121)[&#8202;](#ref-back-122-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-122)

### New Brunswick–Trenton high-speed upgrade

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: New Brunswick–Trenton high-speed upgrade")\]

In August 2011, Congress obligated $450 million to a six-year project to add capacity on one of the busiest segments on the NEC in [New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Jersey "New Jersey").[&#8202;](#ref-back-obligated-1)[\[61\]](#refnote-obligated) The project is designed to upgrade electrical power, signal systems and catenary wires on a 24-mile (39km) section between [New Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Brunswick,_New_Jersey "New Brunswick, New Jersey") and [Trenton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trenton,_New_Jersey "Trenton, New Jersey") to improve reliability, increase speeds up to 160mph (260km/h), and support more frequent high-speed service.[&#8202;](#ref-back-123-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-123)[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-124)[&#8202;](#ref-back-125-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-125) The improvements were scheduled to be completed in 2016, but have been delayed repeatedly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[125\]](#refnote-126) The track work is one of several projects planned for the "New Jersey Speedway" section of the NEC, which include a new station at [North Brunswick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Brunswick_station "North Brunswick station"), the [Mid-Line Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-Line_Loop "Mid-Line Loop") (a flyover for reversing train direction), and the re-construction of [County Yard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_Yard "County Yard"), to be done in coordination with [NJ Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NJ_Transit "NJ Transit").[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-127) _Acela_ trains began operating at speeds up to 150mph (240km/h) between [Princeton Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princeton_Junction_station "Princeton Junction station") and New Brunswick in June 2022. With the planned introduction of the [Avelia Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avelia_Liberty "Avelia Liberty") in 2025, speeds will increase to 160 miles per hour (260km/h).[&#8202;](#ref-back-128-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-128)

### New trains for Acela

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: New trains for Acela")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/First_in-service_Avelia_Liberty_departs_Boston_August_2025_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-First_in-service_Avelia_Liberty_departs_Boston_August_2025_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:First_in-service_Avelia_Liberty_departs_Boston_August_2025_(cropped).jpg) The first in-service Avelia Liberty trainset departing [Boston South Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Station "South Station") on August 28, 2025

Main article: [Avelia Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avelia_Liberty "Avelia Liberty")

On August 26, 2016, Vice President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") announced a $2.45 billion federal loan package to pay for new _Acela_ equipment and upgrades to the NEC. The loans will finance 28 trainsets to replace the existing fleet. The trains are being built by [Alstom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alstom "Alstom") in [Hornell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hornell,_New_York "Hornell, New York") and [Rochester, New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rochester,_New_York "Rochester, New York"). Passenger service using the new trains had been expected to begin in 2024, but implementation was highly delayed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-129-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-129) The first new trainsets entered service on August 28, 2025.[&#8202;](#ref-back-130-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-130)[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-131) The current fleet is expected to be retired when all the replacements have been delivered. Amtrak plans to repay the loans through increased NEC passenger revenue.[&#8202;](#ref-back-132-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-132)

### Connecticut speed upgrade

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=42 "Edit section: Connecticut speed upgrade")\]

The [Connecticut Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut_Department_of_Transportation "Connecticut Department of Transportation") plans to replace a three-mile stretch of track in [Bridgeport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridgeport,_Connecticut "Bridgeport, Connecticut"), [Stratford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stratford,_Connecticut "Stratford, Connecticut"), and [Milford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milford,_Connecticut "Milford, Connecticut"), including the reconstruction of eight bridges (one in Bridgeport and seven in Stratford), catenary replacement, the reconstruction of one [interlocking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interlocking "Interlocking") for high-speed track changes, and the construction of an additional new high-speed interlocking.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ctpost-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-ctpost)[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-134) These improvements will allow Metro-North and Amtrak trains to travel at 90mph (140km/h) instead of the current 70mph (110km/h) limit.[&#8202;](#ref-back-135-0)[\[134\]](#refnote-135)[&#8202;](#ref-back-136-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-136) The $385 million project is planned to begin in 2025, with completion estimated in December 2028.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ctpost-1)[\[132\]](#refnote-ctpost)[&#8202;](#ref-back-fsp-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-fsp)

### Replacement of bridge over Housatonic River

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=43 "Edit section: Replacement of bridge over Housatonic River")\]

Main article: [Housatonic River Railroad Bridge §Replacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housatonic_River_Railroad_Bridge#Replacement "Housatonic River Railroad Bridge")

In [Milford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milford,_Connecticut "Milford, Connecticut") and [Stratford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stratford,_Connecticut "Stratford, Connecticut"), [Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut "Connecticut"), the 1906 [Devon Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housatonic_River_Railroad_Bridge "Housatonic River Railroad Bridge") over the [Housatonic River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housatonic_River "Housatonic River") causes delays and speed restrictions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-devonbridge-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-devonbridge) The four-track [bascule bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bascule_bridge "Bascule bridge") is planned to be rehabilitated and eventually replaced, improving reliability and reducing travel times.[&#8202;](#ref-back-139-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-139) The Connecticut Department of Transportation was awarded $119.3 million for interim repairs to the bridge in 2023.[&#8202;](#ref-back-fsp-1)[\[136\]](#refnote-fsp) Repairs are scheduled to run from June 2025 until June 2027. A new bridge to replace the existing span is to be built from October 2030 to August 2036.[&#8202;](#ref-back-devonbridge-1)[\[137\]](#refnote-devonbridge) Amtrak was awarded up to $246 million in [Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure_Investment_and_Jobs_Act "Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act") funds in November 2023 for the design of a replacement for the span. Amtrak will contribute an additional $16 million, while the state of Connecticut will provide $45 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-fsp-2)[\[136\]](#refnote-fsp) The replacement bridge is being designed for a top speed of 70mph (110km/h), up from the current 40mph (64km/h) limit on the existing bridge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-fragrant-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-fragrant)

### Replacement of bridge over Hutchinson River

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=44 "Edit section: Replacement of bridge over Hutchinson River")\]

Main article: [Pelham Bay Bridge §Bridge replacement project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelham_Bay_Bridge#Bridge_replacement_project "Pelham Bay Bridge")

Amtrak is planning to replace the 1907 low-level movable rail [Pelham Bay Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelham_Bay_Bridge "Pelham Bay Bridge") (just west of [Pelham Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelham_Bridge "Pelham Bridge")) over the [Hutchinson River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutchinson_River "Hutchinson River") in [the Bronx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bronx "The Bronx") that has been limiting speed and train capacity. The goal is for a new bridge to support expanded service and speeds up to 70mph (110km/h).[&#8202;](#ref-back-pelhambay-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-pelhambay)[&#8202;](#ref-back-142-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-142) Construction is anticipated to begin in 2029, with completion expected in 2034.[&#8202;](#ref-back-pelhambay-1)[\[140\]](#refnote-pelhambay)[&#8202;](#ref-back-pelhamamtrak-0)[\[142\]](#refnote-pelhamamtrak) Funding for the replacement bridge will be provided by the [Metropolitan Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Transportation_Authority "Metropolitan Transportation Authority").[&#8202;](#ref-back-pelhamamtrak-1)[\[142\]](#refnote-pelhamamtrak)

### Replacement of bridge over Mianus River

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=45 "Edit section: Replacement of bridge over Mianus River")\]

Main article: [Mianus River Railroad Bridge §Replacement Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mianus_River_Railroad_Bridge#Replacement_Project "Mianus River Railroad Bridge")

In the November of 2024, the [Connecticut Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut_Department_of_Transportation "Connecticut Department of Transportation") was awarded a $6.4 Million federal grant to explore options to find a replacement for the 1904-built [Mianus River Railroad Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mianus_River_Railroad_Bridge "Mianus River Railroad Bridge") (also known as the [Cos Cob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cos_Cob,_Connecticut "Cos Cob, Connecticut") Bridge) in [Greenwich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwich,_Connecticut "Greenwich, Connecticut"), [Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connecticut "Connecticut").[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-144)

### Replacement of bridge over Norwalk River

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=46 "Edit section: Replacement of bridge over Norwalk River")\]

Main article: [Norwalk River Railroad Bridge §Replacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwalk_River_Railroad_Bridge#Replacement "Norwalk River Railroad Bridge")

In May 2023, construction began to replace the 1896 [Walk Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwalk_River_Railroad_Bridge "Norwalk River Railroad Bridge"), a [swing bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_bridge "Swing bridge") over the [Norwalk River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwalk_River "Norwalk River") in [Norwalk, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwalk,_Connecticut "Norwalk, Connecticut"). The current bridge is a frequent point of failure and a source of speed restrictions for Amtrak and Metro-North trains.[&#8202;](#ref-back-145-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-145) The replacement bridge, a dual-span [lift bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertical-lift_bridge "Vertical-lift bridge"), is expected to be completed in 2029.[&#8202;](#ref-back-146-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-146)[&#8202;](#ref-back-147-0)[\[146\]](#refnote-147) The replacement bridge is designed for a speed increase of 15mph (24km/h).[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-148) Amtrak was awarded $465 million in [Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure_Investment_and_Jobs_Act "Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act") funds in November 2023. Amtrak will contribute an additional $27 million, while the state of Connecticut will provide $87 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FSP-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-FSP)[&#8202;](#ref-back-150-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-150)

### Replacement of bridge over Saugatuck River

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Northeast_Corridor&action=edit&section=47 "Edit section: Replacement of bridge over Saugatuck River")\]

Main article: [Saugatuck River Railroad Bridge §Replacement Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saugatuck_River_Railroad_Bridge#Replacement_Project "Saugatuck River Railroad Bridge")

In [Westport, Connecticut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westport,_Connecticut "Westport, Connecticut"), the 1905-built [Saugatuck River Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saugatuck_River_Railroad_Bridge "Saugatuck River Railroad Bridge") over the [Saugatuck River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saugatuck_River "Saugatuck River") is planned to be replaced with a new span, as the current four-track bascule bridge is a source of delays and speed restrictions.[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-151) Construction on the replacement bridge is expected to last from January 2030 to October 2033.[&#8202;](#ref-back-fragrant-1)[\[139\]](#refnote-fragrant) The replacement bridge is being designed for a top speed of 90mph (140km/h), up from the current 45mph (72km/h) limit on the existing bridge.[&#8202;](#ref-back-fragrant-2)[\[139\]](#refnote-fragrant) Amtrak was awarded up to $23.2 million in Infrastructure Investment and Jobs Act funds in November 2023 for early design of a replacement for the span. Amtrak will contribute an additional $1.6 million, while the state of Connecticut will provide $4.2 million.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FSP-1)[\[148\]](#refnote-FSP)
